Founded by physician Tom Lee in San Francisco in 2007, One Medical operates as a direct-to-consumer and B2B primary care service across 19+ major U.S. cities with over 125 offices. The company also acquired Iora Health in September 2021, adding 47 offices serving Medicare patients.\n     9→\n    10→One Medical's business model combines an annual membership fee ($99/year for Amazon Prime members; higher for non-Prime) with standard insurance billing for clinical visits. They partner with over 8,500 companies to offer One Medical as an employee health benefit. The practice employs board-certified physicians, nurse practitioners, and physician assistants specializing in family medicine and internal medicine. One Medical reports a 90+ Net Promoter Score, with 45% of members using digital services monthly.\n    11→\n    12→Crucially, One Medical built its own proprietary EHR platform called **1Life** from the ground up — they do not use a third-party EHR like Epic or athenahealth. This makes the certified \"1Life\" product both the EHR and essentially the entire technology backbone of the One Medical clinical operation.\n    13→\n    14→## Product: 1Life\n    15→\n    16→CHPL IDs: 10964\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→1Life is One Medical's proprietary, purpose-built health IT platform. It is not a standalone EHR sold to third parties — it is the internal technology system that powers all of One Medical's clinical, administrative, and patient-facing operations. The certified module \"1Life\" is the EHR component, but the broader 1Life ecosystem encompasses the entire technology stack for running One Medical's primary care practice.\n    21→\n    22→The platform was built on AWS from the company's founding in 2007, using a modern tech stack including Rails, React, GraphQL, and OpenSearch/ElasticSearch. It supports FHIR for interoperability and incorporates machine learning for workflow optimization. SureScripts is listed as relied-upon software in the ONC certification, indicating electronic prescribing integration.\n    23→\n    24→Certified criteria include clinical documentation (a)(1), demographics (a)(5), family health history (a)(12), implantable device list (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1), patient health information request (e)(3), and FHIR-based API access (g)(10). Notably absent from certification: CPOE criteria (a)(2)/(a)(3), clinical decision support (a)(9), and public health reporting (f) criteria. The intended users are described as \"ambulatory physicians and nurses.\"\n    25→\n    26→### Users & Market\n    27→\n    28→One Medical is a direct provider of primary care — the end users of 1Life are:\n    29→- **Clinicians**: Physicians, nurse practitioners, and physician assistants who chart, order labs, prescribe medications, and document visits\n    30→- **Care team members**: Nurses, care navigators, and health coaches who manage chronic care programs, handle tasks, and coordinate referrals\n    31→- **Administrative staff**: Handle scheduling, insurance, billing, and referral logistics\n    32→- **Patients/Members**: Access the patient-facing portal and mobile apps (iOS/Android) for scheduling, messaging, prescription renewals, lab results, and virtual visits\n    33→\n    34→The clinical setting is exclusively ambulatory primary care — no hospitals, no specialty practices, no surgical centers. One Medical operates its own clinics with employed providers rather than licensing the software to external practices. The company has over 125 offices across cities including San Francisco, New York, Seattle, Los Angeles, Chicago, Boston, Atlanta, and others.\n    35→\n    36→Notable partnerships include collaborations with Cleveland Clinic (October 2024) and Montefiore Health System to coordinate primary and specialty care. Amazon has also been integrating AI features, including a Health AI assistant launched in early 2025 that can connect members to providers, review lab results, and manage medications.\n    37→\n Founded by physician Tom Lee in San Francisco in 2007, One Medical operates as a direct-to-consumer and B2B primary care service across 19+ major U.S. cities with over 125 offices. The company also acquired Iora Health in September 2021, adding 47 offices serving Medicare patients.\n\nOne Medical's business model combines an annual membership fee ($99/year for Amazon Prime members; higher for non-Prime) with standard insurance billing for clinical visits. They partner with over 8,500 companies to offer One Medical as an employee health benefit. The practice employs board-certified physicians, nurse practitioners, and physician assistants specializing in family medicine and internal medicine. One Medical reports a 90+ Net Promoter Score, with 45% of members using digital services monthly.\n\nCrucially, One Medical built its own proprietary EHR platform called **1Life** from the ground up — they do not use a third-party EHR like Epic or athenahealth. This makes the certified \"1Life\" product both the EHR and essentially the entire technology backbone of the One Medical clinical operation.\n\n## Product: 1Life\n\nCHPL IDs: 10964\n\n### What It Is\n\n1Life is One Medical's proprietary, purpose-built health IT platform. It is not a standalone EHR sold to third parties — it is the internal technology system that powers all of One Medical's clinical, administrative, and patient-facing operations. The certified module \"1Life\" is the EHR component, but the broader 1Life ecosystem encompasses the entire technology stack for running One Medical's primary care practice.\n\nThe platform was built on AWS from the company's founding in 2007, using a modern tech stack including Rails, React, GraphQL, and OpenSearch/ElasticSearch. It supports FHIR for interoperability and incorporates machine learning for workflow optimization. SureScripts is listed as relied-upon software in the ONC certification, indicating electronic prescribing integration.\n\nCertified criteria include clinical documentation (a)(1), demographics (a)(5), family health history (a)(12), implantable device list (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1), patient health information request (e)(3), and FHIR-based API access (g)(10). Notably absent from certification: CPOE criteria (a)(2)/(a)(3), clinical decision support (a)(9), and public health reporting (f) criteria. The intended users are described as \"ambulatory physicians and nurses.\"\n\n### Users & Market\n\nOne Medical is a direct provider of primary care — the end users of 1Life are:\n- **Clinicians**: Physicians, nurse practitioners, and physician assistants who chart, order labs, prescribe medications, and document visits\n- **Care team members**: Nurses, care navigators, and health coaches who manage chronic care programs, handle tasks, and coordinate referrals\n- **Administrative staff**: Handle scheduling, insurance, billing, and referral logistics\n- **Patients/Members**: Access the patient-facing portal and mobile apps (iOS/Android) for scheduling, messaging, prescription renewals, lab results, and virtual visits\n\nThe clinical setting is exclusively ambulatory primary care — no hospitals, no specialty practices, no surgical centers. One Medical operates its own clinics with employed providers rather than licensing the software to external practices. The company has over 125 offices across cities including San Francisco, New York, Seattle, Los Angeles, Chicago, Boston, Atlanta, and others.\n\nNotable partnerships include collaborations with Cleveland Clinic (October 2024) and Montefiore Health System to coordinate primary and specialty care. Amazon has also been integrating AI features, including a Health AI assistant launched in early 2025 that can connect members to providers, review lab results, and manage medications.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials, the AWS case study, and the One Medical technology blog, the 1Life ecosystem includes the following integrated components:\n\n**EHR & Clinical Charting**\n- Clinical documentation system designed collaboratively with clinicians\n- Patient records accessible across all One Medical offices and virtual visits\n- Unified patient profiles with \"all relevant information about each patient\"\n- Support for note-writing, medication prescribing, test ordering, and outside record review\n- Family health history tracking (certified (a)(12))\n- Implantable device list management (certified (a)(14))\n\n**Scheduling Platform**\n- Online appointment booking, rescheduling, and cancellation\n- Same-day and next-day appointment availability\n- Support for 24-72 hours for patient messages\n\n**Virtual Care / Telehealth**\n- 24/7 video chat for urgent concerns (\"Treat-Me-Now\" on-demand visits)\n- Scheduled video visits with providers\n- Photo sharing capability for clinical assessment (e.g., rashes)\n\n**Patient Portal & Mobile Apps**\n- Web portal (app.onemedical.com) and native iOS/Android apps\n- View lab results, request prescription renewals, order management and results delivery (typically 7-10 business days)\n- Provider interpretation of results\n\n**Referral & Care Coordination**\n- Specialist referral requests through the portal\n- Coordination with hospitals and specialty practices\n- Care coordination across One Medical's office network\n\n**Chronic Care Management (Impact by One Medical)**\n- Team-based chronic condition management program\n- Personalized care plans with 1:1 coaching and group classes\n- Care navigators and health coaches\n- Integration with specialist coordination at health system partners\n\n**Administrative & Billing**\n- Insurance verification and billing (accepts most major carriers and Medicare)\n- Membership management\n- Administrative support through messaging\n\n**Transitions of Care**\n- C-CDA document generation and consumption (certified (b)(1))\n- Health information exchange capabilities\n\n**API & Interoperability**\n- FHIR R4 API for patient and population access (certified (g)(10))\n- FHIR-based data exchange supporting standard resource types\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features described above and the certification features)\n- Allergy lists (certified (a)(1))\n- Family health history (certified (a)(12))\n- Implantable device lists (certified (a)(14))\n- Lab orders and results (on-site lab services, results in // Skip header row\n      }\n\n      if (cells.length >= 4) {\n        rows.push({\n          name: cells[0],\n          type: cells[1],\n          cardinality: cells[2],\n          description: cells[3],\n        });\n      } else if (cells.length === 2) {\n        // Some tables (like EHI overview) have 2-column layout\n        rows.push({\n          name: cells[0],\n          type: \"\",\n          cardinality: \"\",\n          description: cells[1] || \"\",\n        });\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (rows.length > 0) {\n      tables.push({ fields: rows });\n    }\n  }\n\n  return tables;\n}\n\nfunction extractEhiExportSummary(html: string): EhiExportSummary {\n  const text = stripHtml(html);\n\n  // Extract FHIR resource types from the JSON section table\n  const jsonResourceTypes: string[] = [];\n  const resourceTypeMatch = html.match(\n    /Resource Type<\\/th>[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/i\n  );\n  if (resourceTypeMatch) {\n    const tdRegex = /<td[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>/gi;\n    let m;\n    while ((m = tdRegex.exec(resourceTypeMatch[0])) !== null) {\n      const val = stripHtml(m[1]);\n      if (val) jsonResourceTypes.push(val);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Extract C-CDA sections\n  const ccdaSections: string[] = [];\n  const ccdaMatch = html.match(\n    /C-CDA Section<\\/th>[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/i\n  );\n  if (ccdaMatch) {\n    const tdRegex = /<td[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>/gi;\n    let m;\n    while ((m = tdRegex.exec(ccdaMatch[0])) !== null) {\n      const val = stripHtml(m[1]);\n      if (val) ccdaSections.push(val);\n    }\n  }\n\n  return {\n    description:\n      \"The EHI export contains the electronic health information available for a single patient or multiple patients in computable file formats. C-CDA version 2.1 specification\",\n        ccdaSections,\n      },\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction extractCcdaInfo(html: string): Record<string, unknown> {\n  const text = html;\n\n  // Extract API endpoint info\n  const sections: string[] = [];\n  const sectionMatch = text.match(/C-CDA Section<\\/th>[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/i);\n  if (sectionMatch) {\n    const rowRegex =    requestParameters: params,\n    sections,\n  };\n}\n\nfunction extractExtensions(html: string): Record<string, unknown>[] {\n  const extensions: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n\n  // Parse extension sections - look for h2/h3 headings followed by content\n  // The page has sections: Race, Ethnicity, Machine learning, Patient survey score\n  const sectionRegex =\n    /<h[23][^>]*>([^<]+)<\\/h[23]>([\\s\\S]*?)(?=<h[23]|<footer|$)/gi;\n  let m;\n  while ((m = sectionRegex.exec(html)) !== null) {\n    const title = stripHtml(m[1]);\n    const content = m[2];\n\n    // Extract URL\n    const urlMatch = content.match(\n      /URL:\\s*(?:<[^>]*>)*\\s*(https?:\\/\\/[^\\s<\"]+)/i\n    );\n    // Extract \"Currently used in\"\n    const usedInMatch = content.match(\n      /Currently used in:\\s*(?:<[^>]*>)*\\s*([^<\\n]+)/i\n    );\n\n    if (urlMatch || usedInMatch) {\n      extensions.push({\n        name: title,\n        url: urlMatch ? urlMatch[1] : null,\n extractTerminology(html: string): Record<string, unknown>[] {\n  const terms: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n\n  // Find code/description tables\n  const tableRegex = /<table[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/table>/gi;\n  let tableMatch;\n  // Track which section we're in by looking at the h2/h3 before the table\n  const sectionHeadings: string[] = [];\n  const headingRegex = /<h[23][^>]*>([^<]+)<\\/h[23]>/gi;\n  let hm;\n  while ((hm = headingRegex.exec(html)) !== null) {\n    sectionHeadings.push(stripHtml(hm[1]));\n  }\n\n  let tableIdx = 0;\n  while ((tableMatch = tableRegex.exec(html)) !== null) {\n    const tableHtml = tableMatch[1];\n    const rowRegex = /<tr[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/tr>/gi;\n    let rowMatch;\n    let isFirst = true;\n    while ((rowMatch = Date().toISOString(),\n    totalFilesDiscovered: 0,\n    totalFilesParsed: 0,\n    parseFailures: [],\n    resourceSummary: [],\n  };\n\n  // 1. // Skip header row\n      }\n\n      if (cells.length >= 4) {\n        rows.push({\n          name: cells[0],\n          type: cells[1],\n          cardinality: cells[2],\n          description: cells[3],\n        });\n      } else if (cells.length === 2) {\n        // Some tables (like EHI overview) have 2-column layout\n        rows.push({\n          name: cells[0],\n          type: \"\",\n          cardinality: \"\",\n          description: cells[1] || \"\",\n        });\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (rows.length > 0) {\n      tables.push({ fields: rows });\n    }\n  }\n\n  return tables;\n}\n\nfunction extractEhiExportSummary(html: string): EhiExportSummary {\n  const text = stripHtml(html);\n\n  // Extract FHIR resource types from the JSON section table\n  const jsonResourceTypes: string[] = [];\n  const resourceTypeMatch = html.match(\n    /Resource Type<\\/th>[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/i\n  );\n  if (resourceTypeMatch) {\n    const tdRegex = /<td[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>/gi;\n    let m;\n    while ((m = tdRegex.exec(resourceTypeMatch[0])) !== null) {\n      const val = stripHtml(m[1]);\n      if (val) jsonResourceTypes.push(val);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Extract C-CDA sections\n  const ccdaSections: string[] = [];\n  const ccdaMatch = html.match(\n    /C-CDA Section<\\/th>[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/i\n  );\n  if (ccdaMatch) {\n    const tdRegex = /<td[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>/gi;\n    let m;\n    while ((m = tdRegex.exec(ccdaMatch[0])) !== null) {\n      const val = stripHtml(m[1]);\n      if (val) ccdaSections.push(val);\n    }\n  }\n\n  return {\n    description:\n      \"The EHI export contains the electronic health information available for a single patient or multiple patients in computable file formats. The export(s) can be downloaded as a zip file containing a .json and a .xml file.\",\n    formats: {\n      json: {\n        description:\n          \"FHIR bundle with all resources on record belonging to the patient\",\n        resourceTypes: jsonResourceTypes,\n      },\n      xml: {\n        description:\n          \"Patient Continuity of Care Document formatted in accordance with the C-CDA version 2.1 specification\",\n        ccdaSections,\n      },\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction extractCcdaInfo(html: string): Record<string, unknown> {\n  const text = html;\n\n  // Extract API endpoint info\n  const sections: string[] = [];\n  const sectionMatch = text.match(/C-CDA Section<\\/th>[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/i);\n  if (sectionMatch) {\n    const rowRegex = /<tr[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/tr>/gi;\n    let m;\n    let isFirst = true;\n    while ((m = rowRegex.exec(sectionMatch[0])) !== null) {\n      if (isFirst) { isFirst = false; continue; }\n      const ${stripHtml(cells[1][1])})`\n        );\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Extract request parameters\n  const params: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n  const paramMatch = text.match(/Parameter<\\/th>[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/i);\n  if (paramMatch) {\n    const rowRegex = /<tr[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/tr>/gi;\n    let m;\n    let isFirst = true;\n    while ((m = rowRegex.exec(paramMatch[0])) !== null) {\n      if (isFirst) { isFirst = false; continue; }\n      const cells = [...m[1].matchAll(/<td[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>/gi)];\n      if (cells.length >= 4) stripHtml(cells[3][1]),\n        });\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  return {\n    endpoint: \"GET https://production.app.1life.com/api/ccda\",\n    authentication: \"Basic Auth (registration required)\",\n    requestParameters: params,\n    sections,\n  };\n}\n\nfunction extractExtensions(html: string): Record<string, unknown>[] {\n  const extensions: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n\n  // Parse extension sections - look for h2/h3 headings followed by content\n  // The page has sections: Race, Ethnicity, Machine learning, Patient survey score\n  const sectionRegex =\n    /<h[23][^>]*>([^<]+)<\\/h[23]>([\\s\\S]*?)(?=<h[23]|<footer|$)/gi;\n  let m;\n  while ((m = sectionRegex.exec(html)) !== null) {\n    const title = stripHtml(m[1]);\n    const content = m[2];\n\n    // Extract URL\n    const urlMatch = content.match(\n      /URL:\\s*(?:<[^>]*>)*\\s*(https?:\\/\\/[^\\s<\"]+)/i\n    );\n    // Extract \"Currently used in\"\n    const usedInMatch = content.match(\n      /Currently used in:\\s*(?:<[^>]*>)*\\s*([^<\\n]+)/i\n    );\n\n    if (urlMatch || usedInMatch) {\n      extensions.push({\n        name: title,\n        url: urlMatch ? urlMatch[1] : null,\n        usedIn: usedInMatch ? stripHtml(usedInMatch[1]) : null,\n        description: stripHtml(content).substring(0, 300),\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  return extensions;\n}\n\nfunction extractTerminology(html: string): Record<string, unknown>[] {\n  const terms: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n\n  // Find code/description tables\n  const tableRegex = /<table[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/table>/gi;\n  let tableMatch;\n  // Track which section we're in by looking at the h2/h3 before the table\n  const sectionHeadings: string[] = [];\n  const headingRegex = tableRegex.exec(html)) !== null) {\n    const tableHtml = tableMatch[1];\n    const rowRegex = /<tr[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/tr>/gi;\n    let rowMatch;\n    let isFirst = true;\n    while ((rowMatch = queryable JSON.\n *\n * Usage: bun run extract-fhir-resources.ts\n * Input: ../fhir/resources/*.html, ../fhir/extensions.html, ../fhir/terminology.html,\n *        ../ehi-export-overview.html, }[];\n  resourceSummary: { resourceType: string; fieldCount: number; tableCount: number }[];\n}\n\nfunction stripHtml(html: string): string {\n  return html.replace(/<[^>]+>/g, \"\").replace(/&amp;/g, .replace(/&ndash;/g, \"–\").replace(/&mdash;/g, \"—\").replace(/&#39;/g, \"'\")\n    .replace(/&nbsp;/g, \" \").trim();\n}\n\nfunction extractTables(html: string): { fields: Field[] }[] {\n  const tables: { fields: Field[] }[] = [];\n  const tableRegex = /<table[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/table>/gi;\n  let tableMatch;\n\n  while ((tableMatch = tableRegex.exec(html)) !== null) {\n    const tableHtml = tableMatch[1];\n    const rows: Field[] = [];\n    const rowRegex = /<tr[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/tr>/gi;\n    let rowMatch;\n    let isHeader = true;\n\n    while ((rowMatch = rowRegex.exec(tableHtml)) Some tables (like EHI overview) have 2-column layout\n        rows.push({\n          name: cells[0],\n          type: \"\",\n          cardinality: \"\",\n          description: cells[1] || \"\",\n        });\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (rows.length > 0) {\n      tables.push({ fields: rows });\n    }\n  }\n\n  return tables;\n}\n\nfunction extractEhiExportSummary(html: string): EhiExportSummary {\n  const text = stripHtml(html);\n\n  // Extract FHIR resource types from the JSON section table\n  const jsonResourceTypes: string[] = [];\n  const resourceTypeMatch = html.match(\n    /Resource Type<\\/th>[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/i\n  );\n  if (resourceTypeMatch) {\n    const tdRegex = /<td[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/td>/gi;\n    let m;\n    while ((m = tdRegex.exec(resourceTypeMatch[0])) !== null)  const val = stripHtml(m[1]);\n      if (val) ccdaSections.push(val);\n    }\n  }\n\n  return {\n    description:\n      \"The EHI export contains the electronic health information available for a single patient or multiple patients in computable file formats. The export(s) can be downloaded as a zip file containing a .json and a .xml file.\",\n    formats: {\n      json: {\n        description:\n          \"FHIR bundle with all resources on record belonging to the patient\",\n        resourceTypes: jsonResourceTypes,\n      },\n      xml: {\n        description:\n          \"Patient Continuity of Care Document formatted in accordance with the C-CDA version 2.1 specification\",\n        ccdaSections,\n      },\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction extractCcdaInfo(html: string): Record<string, unknown> {\n  const text = html;\n\n  // Extract API endpoint info\n  const sections: string[] = [];\n  const sectionMatch = text.match(/C-CDA Section<\\/th>[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/i);\n  if (sectionMatch) {\n    const rowRegex = /<tr[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/tr>/gi;\n    let m;\n    let isFirst = true;\n    while ((m = rowRegex.exec(sectionMatch[0])) !== null) {\n      if (isFirst) { isFirst = false; continue; }\n      const error: string }[];\n  resourceSummary: { resourceType: string; fieldCount: number; tableCount: number }[];\n}\n\nfunction stripHtml(html: string): string {\n  return html.replace(/<[^>]+>/g, \"\").replace(/&amp;/g, \"&\").replace(/&lt;/g, \"<\")\n    .replace(/&gt;/g, \">\").replace(/&quot;/g, '\"').replace(/&#34;/g, '\"')\n    .replace(/&rsquo;/g, \"'\").replace(/&ldquo;/g, '\"').replace(/&rdquo;/g, '\"')\n    .replace(/&ndash;/g, \"–\").replace(/&mdash;/g, \"—\").replace(/&#39;/g, \"'\")\n    .replace(/&nbsp;/g, \" \").trim();\n}\n\nfunction extractTables(html: string): { fields: Field[] }[] {\n  const tables: { fields: Field[] }[] = [];\n  const tableRegex = /<table[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/table>/gi;\n  let tableMatch;\n\n  while ((tableMatch = tableRegex.exec(html)) !== null) {\n    const tableHtml = tableMatch[1];\n    const rows: Field[] = [];\n    const rowRegex = /<tr[^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/tr>/gi;\n    let rowMatch;\n    let isHeader = true;\n\n    while ((rowMatch = rowRegex.exec(tableHtml)) !== null) {\n      const cellRegex = /<t[dh][^>]*>([\\s\\S]*?)<\\/t[dh]>/gi;\n      const cells: string[] = [];\n      let cellMatch;\n      while ((cellMatch = cellRegex.exec(rowMatch[1])) !== null) {\n        cells.push(stripHtml(cellMatch[1]));\n      }\n\n      if (isHeader) {\n        isHeader = false;\n        continue; The export(s) can be downloaded as a zip file containing a .json and a .xml file.\",\n    formats: {\n      json: {\n        description:\n          \"FHIR bundle with all resources on record C-CDA version 2.1 specification\",\n        ccdaSections,\n      },\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction extractCcdaInfo(html: string): Record<string, unknown> {\n  const text = html;\n\n  // Extract API endpoint info\n  const sections: string[] = [];\n  const sectionMatch = text.match(/C-CDA Section<\\/th>[\\s\\S]*?<\\/table>/i);\n  if (sectionMatch) {\n    const rowRegex = ${stripHtml(cells[1][1])})`\n        );\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Extract request parameters\n  const params: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n  const paramMatch =    requestParameters: params,\n    sections,\n  };\n}\n\nfunction extractExtensions(html: string): Record<string, unknown>[] {\n  const extensions: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n\n  // Hugo generates headings like: <h4 id=race>Race\\n<a ...>...</a></h4>\n  // Split into sections by h3/h4 headings\n  const sectionRegex =\n const terms: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n\n  // Hugo headings have anchors inside: <h2 id=consent>Consent\\n<a ...>\n  // Find heading+table pairs - each heading precedes a table\n  const return terms;\n}\n\nasync function main() {\n  const report: ExtractionReport = {\n    extractedAt: new Date().toISOString(),\n    totalFilesDiscovered: 0,\n    totalFilesParsed: 0,\n    parseFailures: [],\n    resourceSummary: [],\n  };\n\n  // 1. = null;\n  let extensionsData: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n  let terminologyData: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n  let ccdaData: Record<string, unknown> = {};\n\n  for (const file of additionalFiles) {\n    try {\n      const html = await readFile(join(DOWNLOADS_DIR, file), \"utf-8\");\n      report.totalFilesParsed++;\n\n      if (file === \"ehi-export-overview.html\") {\n        ehiSummary = extractEhiExportSummary(html);\n      } else if (file === \"fhir/extensions.html\") {\n        extensionsData = extractExtensions(html);\n      } else if (file === console.log(`  FHIR resources extracted: ${resources.length}`);\n  console.log(\n    `  Total fields across all resources: ${resources.reduce((s, r) => s + r.totalFields, 0)}`\n The site is a static Hugo-generated documentation portal. No redirects. Content-Length: 37,082 bytes.\n\n**Step 2: Downloaded the main EHI export page**\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://apidocs.onemedical.io/ehi_export/overview/\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o downloads/ehi-export-overview.html\n```\nThe page contains the EHI export overview with two tables: one listing FHIR resource types included in the JSON export, and one listing C-CDA sections in the XML export. No downloadable files (PDF, ZIP, JSON schema, etc.) linked anywhere on the page.\n\n**Step 3: Mapped the full site via sitemap.xml**\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://apidocs.onemedical.io/sitemap.xml\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0'\n```\nThe sitemap revealed 43 URLs across four sections:\n- **EHI Export**: 2 pages (index + overview)\n- **FHIR**: 28 pages (overview, capability statement, authentication, 22 resource pages, search/pagination, extensions, terminology)\n- **C-CDA**: 2 pages (index + patient continuity of care document)\n- **Legal**: 5 pages (terms, certification info, real world test plan, risk management, service base URL)\n\n**Step 4: Downloaded all documentation pages**\n\nSince the EHI export page states the JSON file is a FHIR bundle, the FHIR resource documentation pages define the export schema. All 22 FHIR resource pages were downloaded, along with the FHIR overview, capability statement, extensions, and terminology pages. The C-CDA documentation page was also downloaded as the EHI export includes a C-CDA XML file.\n\nAll pages downloaded via curl with `User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0` header. No JavaScript required — the site is static HTML generated by Hugo.\n\n**Step 5: Checked for downloadable artifacts**\n\nSearched all downloaded HTML pages for links to downloadable files (PDF, ZIP, JSON, XLSX, CSV, XML, YAML, XSD, OpenAPI specs). Found none. The entire documentation is HTML only — no machine-readable schema files, no sample exports, no OpenAPI specifications.\n\n**Step 6: Screenshot**\n\nTook a full-page screenshot of the EHI export overview page using Chrome DevTools: `downloads/screenshot-ehi-export-overview.png`\n\n**Step 7: Enrichment**\n\nCreated a Bun TypeScript extraction script that parses all 28 HTML files into structured JSON. Outputs:\n- `fhir-resources.json`: 22 resources, 629 fields total\n- `ehi-export-summary.json`: EHI export format, C-CDA sections, extensions, terminology\n- `extraction-report.json`: accounting/coverage data\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### EHI Export Format\n\nThe EHI export produces a **ZIP file** containing two files per patient:\n\n1. **`.json` file**: A FHIR R4 Bundle containing all resources on record for the patient. The documentation lists 17 FHIR resource types that \"may be included\":\n   - AllergyIntolerance, CareTeam, Communication, Condition (listed twice — likely a typo), Consent, Device, DiagnosticReport, DocumentReference, Encounter, Goal, Immunization, MedicationRequest, Observation, Patient, Procedure, ServiceRequest\n\n2. **`.xml` file**: A C-CDA v2.1 Patient Continuity of Care Document with 18 sections:\n   - Allergies, Assessment and Plan, Care Team, Encounters, Family History, Goals, Health Concerns, Immunizations, Medical Equipment, Medications, Notes, Patient, Plan of Treatment, Problems, Procedures, Results, Social History, Vital Signs\n\nThe export supports single patient or multiple patients. No API documentation is provided for how the export is triggered — the page describes only the output format.\n\n### FHIR Resource Documentation (Field-Level Detail)\n\nThe FHIR section of the site provides detailed field-level documentation for 22 resource types. Each resource page has a table with:\n- **Field Name** (including dotted sub-paths like `.coding.system`)\n- **Field Type** (Text, code, CodeableConcept, Reference, etc.)\n- **Cardinality** (0..1, 1..1, 0..*, etc.)\n- **Description and bindings** (including value set bindings and One Medical-specific notes)\n\nThe 22 documented resource types with field counts:\n- Patient (60 fields, 2 tables including extensions), Observation (48 fields), DocumentReference (42 fields), Immunization (36 fields), DiagnosticReport (35 fields), Procedure (34 fields), Encounter (33 fields), Coverage (31 fields), Questionnaire (31 fields), ServiceRequest (30 fields), AllergyIntolerance (27 fields), CareTeam (26 fields), Location (26 fields), CarePlan (24 fields), Condition (23 fields), QuestionnaireResponse (23 fields), MedicationRequest (21 fields), Practitioner (19 fields), Organization (18 fields), Provenance (17 fields), Medication (13 fields), Device (12 fields)\n\n**Notable: The EHI export page lists resources not documented in the FHIR section**: Communication, Consent, and Goal have no individual resource documentation pages. Conversely, the FHIR section documents resources not listed on the EHI export page: CarePlan, Coverage, Location, Medication, Organization, Practitioner, Provenance, Questionnaire, QuestionnaireResponse. These are likely referenced/supporting resources in the FHIR bundle.\n\n### C-CDA API Documentation\n\nThe C-CDA section documents a separate API endpoint for generating Continuity of Care Documents:\n- **Endpoint**: `GET https://production.app.1life.com/api/ccda`\n- **Authentication**: Basic Auth (registration with 1Life required)\n- **Parameters**: `patient_id` (required), `start_date` (optional), `end_date` (optional)\n- Some C-CDA sections respect date range filtering; others always return all data\n\n### Extensions\n\nOne Medical documents 11 FHIR extensions:\n- **US Core**: Race and Ethnicity (on Patient resource)\n- **Machine Learning**: A complex extension with sub-extensions (source type, model name, model repository, model type, model use case, model performance, prediction characteristics) — used on Observation and Procedure resources. Resources with ML extensions are noted as not authoritative and typically have preliminary/draft status.\n- **First Party Data**: Used on Procedure resources\n\nThe ML extensions use URLs pointing to One Medical's internal Google Sites knowledge base (not publicly accessible).\n\n### Custom Terminology\n\nOne Medical defines 3 custom codes under `http://onemedical.com/terminology`:\n- **Consent.TOS**: Terms of Service consent form (signed by new members)\n- **Communication.TMN**: Treat Me Now — secure message threads from on-demand visits\n- **Communication.AVS**: After Visit Summary — post-encounter follow-up instructions\n\n### FHIR API Infrastructure\n\n- Root URLs: `https://api.prod.1life.com/fhir/4.0` (1Life Production), `https://api.stage.1life.com/fhir/4.0` (Testing), and a Chirp/HealthLake URL\n- Media type: `application/fhir+json`\n- CapabilityStatement available at `GET /4.0/metadata`\n- Extensions search parameter supported: `extension=[url]`\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe EHI export uses FHIR R4 as its format. The FHIR resource types included broadly map to **standard clinical data domains** well:\n\n**Clearly covered domains:**\n- Demographics (Patient)\n- Conditions/Problem lists (Condition)\n- Medications/Prescriptions (MedicationRequest, Medication)\n- Allergies (AllergyIntolerance)\n- Lab results (DiagnosticReport, Observation)\n- Vital signs (Observation)\n- Immunizations (Immunization)\n- Procedures (Procedure)\n- Encounters/Visits (Encounter)\n- Care plans and goals (CarePlan, Goal, CareTeam)\n- Clinical notes/documents (DocumentReference)\n- Devices/Implants (Device)\n- Referrals (ServiceRequest)\n- Insurance/Coverage (Coverage)\n- Consent records (Consent)\n- Questionnaire responses (Questionnaire, QuestionnaireResponse) — this is notable as it could capture custom screening forms and assessments\n- Secure messaging/communications (Communication) — includes Treat Me Now and After Visit Summary threads\n\n**Domains with unclear or missing coverage:**\n- **Billing data**: No mention of billing records, charges, claims, or payment information. Coverage (insurance) is documented, but actual billing transactions (charges, claim submissions, EOBs) are absent. For a primary care practice that bills insurance for every visit, this is a significant gap in the EHI export.\n- **Family health history**: Listed in the C-CDA sections but not as a FHIR resource type. It may be embedded in Observation or another resource, but there's no explicit documentation.\n- **Social history**: Listed in C-CDA sections but only \"Social History (Smoking Status)\" is mentioned. Broader social determinants of health are not explicitly documented.\n- **Patient-provider messaging content**: Communication resource is listed, which is good. However, the documentation for Communication has no individual resource page, so the granularity of what's exported (full message threads? just metadata?) is unclear.\n- **Virtual visit / telehealth records**: Not explicitly mentioned. Presumably captured as Encounters with a virtual visit type, but not documented.\n- **Photos/attachments from virtual visits**: May be in DocumentReference, but not explicitly described.\n- **Chronic care management data** (Impact by One Medical): Care plans and goals are present, which may cover this, but care navigator notes and coaching records are not explicitly mentioned.\n\n### The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Question\n\nThis is a **critical finding**: The EHI export documentation is essentially a thin wrapper around One Medical's FHIR API documentation. The resource types listed on the EHI export page (17 types) are a subset of the FHIR API resource types documented in detail (22 types). The FHIR API documentation itself is clearly the (g)(10) API documentation — it describes authentication, search parameters, pagination, and all the standard US Core resources.\n\nHowever, One Medical's approach is more thoughtful than a simple \"our FHIR API is our EHI export\" conflation:\n\n1. **The EHI export includes non-US Core resources**: Communication, Consent, Goal, Questionnaire, and QuestionnaireResponse go beyond the standard US Core/USCDI scope. This suggests One Medical has thought about what additional data to include.\n2. **The export produces a complete FHIR bundle per patient** — not a query-by-query API, but a single downloadable file.\n3. **The C-CDA complement adds sections** (Family History, Social History, Notes) that provide additional clinical context.\n\nThat said, the export still fundamentally operates within the FHIR data model, which means **any data that doesn't map cleanly to FHIR resources is likely missing**. For a proprietary EHR built from scratch, there is almost certainly internal data (billing transactions, internal task queues for care navigation, membership data, referral tracking details) that has no natural FHIR representation and appears absent from the export.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Format**: FHIR R4 JSON Bundle + C-CDA v2.1 XML, delivered as a ZIP\n- **Standards compliance**: The JSON uses FHIR R4 with US Core profiles. The XML uses C-CDA v2.1.\n- **Appropriateness**: FHIR R4 is appropriate for the clinical data this primary care platform manages. The dual-format approach (FHIR + C-CDA) provides both machine-readable structure and a standardized clinical document format.\n- **Reconstructability**: A third party could reconstruct most of the clinical patient record from this export. The detailed field-level documentation (629 fields across 22 resources) provides sufficient schema information. However, relationships between resources rely on FHIR reference mechanics, which are standard but require FHIR expertise to navigate. The absence of billing data means the financial record cannot be reconstructed.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The documentation is well-organized in a clean Hugo-based doc portal\n- Field-level detail is thorough: every resource has a table with field name, type, cardinality, and description\n- Custom extensions and terminology are explicitly documented\n- The C-CDA API has clear endpoint documentation with parameters and response codes\n- 629 total fields documented across 22 resource types is substantial\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- **No sample export files**: There are no example FHIR bundles or C-CDA documents. A developer implementing an import would have to construct expected outputs from the field tables alone.\n- **No export trigger documentation**: The EHI export page describes the output format but says nothing about how to request or trigger an export. Is it an API call? A button in the UI? A request to support? This is a significant omission.\n- **Three resource types undocumented**: Communication, Consent, and Goal are listed as EHI export resource types but have no documentation pages.\n- **No value set documentation for coded fields**: While the Terminology page documents 3 custom codes, many fields reference standard terminologies (SNOMED, LOINC, ICD-10) without specifying which value sets or which codes One Medical actually uses.\n- **Machine learning extension URLs point to internal Google Sites**: The extension URLs (`sites.google.com/onemedical.com/...`) are not publicly accessible, so the full extension specifications cannot be verified externally.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Good — field names, data types, cardinality, and descriptions for most fields\n- **Value sets**: Partially documented. Custom codes have a dedicated Terminology page, but standard code system bindings are mentioned in descriptions without specifying allowed values\n- **Relationships**: Implicit via FHIR references (e.g., Encounter references Patient, Observation references Encounter). The reference targets are documented in field descriptions.\n- **Versioning**: No change history or versioning visible in the documentation. The site's `Last-Modified` header shows `2026-02-12`, so it was recently updated.\n- **Condition listed twice**: The EHI export overview lists \"Condition\" twice in the FHIR resource types table — this appears to be a documentation error.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://apidocs.onemedical.io/ehi_export/overview/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML, no JavaScript required)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (EHI export docs on the registered URL)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n- **No downloadable artifacts**: The entire documentation site is HTML only. No PDF, ZIP, JSON schema, OpenAPI spec, or any machine-readable format beyond the HTML pages themselves. The enrichment extraction script was created to bridge this gap.\n- **Legal pages empty**: The legal directory downloaded but contained no relevant EHI documentation.\n- **Internal knowledge base links**: The FHIR extensions page references `sites.google.com/onemedical.com/technology-knowledge-base/...` for ML extension definitions — these are internal and not publicly accessible.\n- **No sample data**: Despite documenting a complex FHIR bundle export format, no sample JSON or XML files are provided anywhere on the site.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2034,"cache_read_input_tokens":81912,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2034},"output_tokens":7,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ad4de706-f6d6-4b28-8f33-190d857c8afa","uuid":"728817e6-fa40-4643-bc73-8e46bda454d8"}

The FHIR API documentation itself is clearly the (g)(10) API documentation — it describes authentication, search parameters, pagination, and all the standard US Core resources.\n\nHowever, One Medical's approach is more thoughtful than a simple \"our FHIR API is our EHI export\" conflation:\n\n1. **The EHI export includes non-US Core resources**: Communication, Consent, Goal, Questionnaire, and QuestionnaireResponse go beyond the standard US Core/USCDI scope. This suggests One Medical has thought about what additional data to include.\n2. **The export produces a complete FHIR bundle per patient** — not a query-by-query API, but a single downloadable file.\n3. **The C-CDA complement adds sections** (Family History, Social History, Notes) that provide additional clinical context.\n\nThat said, the export still fundamentally operates within the FHIR data model, which means **any data that doesn't map cleanly to FHIR resources is likely missing**. For a proprietary EHR built from scratch, there is almost certainly internal data (billing transactions, internal task queues for care navigation, membership data, referral tracking details) that has no natural FHIR representation and appears absent from the export.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Format**: FHIR R4 JSON Bundle + C-CDA v2.1 XML, delivered as a ZIP\n- **Standards compliance**: The JSON uses FHIR R4 with US Core profiles. The XML uses C-CDA v2.1.\n- **Appropriateness**: FHIR R4 is appropriate for the clinical data this primary care platform manages. The dual-format approach (FHIR + C-CDA) provides both machine-readable structure and a standardized clinical document format.\n- **Reconstructability**: A third party could reconstruct most of the clinical patient record from this export. The detailed field-level documentation (629 fields across 22 resources) provides sufficient schema information. However, relationships between resources rely on FHIR reference mechanics, which are standard but require FHIR expertise to navigate. The absence of billing data means the financial record cannot be reconstructed.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The documentation is well-organized in a clean Hugo-based doc portal\n- Field-level detail is thorough: every resource has a table with field name, type, cardinality, and description\n- Custom extensions and terminology are explicitly documented\n- The C-CDA API has clear endpoint documentation with parameters and response codes\n- 629 total fields documented across 22 resource types is substantial\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- **No sample export files**: There are no example FHIR bundles or C-CDA documents. A developer implementing an import would have to construct expected outputs from the field tables alone.\n- **No export trigger documentation**: The EHI export page describes the output format but says nothing about how to request or trigger an export. Is it an API call? A button in the UI? A request to support? This is a significant omission.\n- **Three resource types undocumented**: Communication, Consent, and Goal are listed as EHI export resource types but have no documentation pages.\n- **No value set documentation for coded fields**: While the Terminology page documents 3 custom codes, many fields reference standard terminologies (SNOMED, LOINC, ICD-10) without specifying which value sets or which codes One Medical actually uses.\n- **Machine learning extension URLs point to internal Google Sites**: The extension URLs (`sites.google.com/onemedical.com/...`) are not publicly accessible, so the full extension specifications cannot be verified externally.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Good — field names, data types, cardinality, and descriptions for most fields\n- **Value sets**: Partially documented. Custom codes have a dedicated Terminology page, but standard code system bindings are mentioned in descriptions without specifying allowed values\n- **Relationships**: Implicit via FHIR references (e.g., Encounter references Patient, Observation references Encounter). The reference targets are documented in field descriptions.\n- **Versioning**: No change history or versioning visible in the documentation. The site's `Last-Modified` header shows `2026-02-12`, so it was recently updated.\n- **Condition listed twice**: The EHI export overview lists \"Condition\" twice in the FHIR resource types table — this appears to be a documentation error.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://apidocs.onemedical.io/ehi_export/overview/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML, no JavaScript required)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (EHI export docs on the registered URL)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n- **No downloadable artifacts**: The entire documentation site is HTML only. No PDF, ZIP, JSON schema, OpenAPI spec, or any machine-readable format beyond the HTML pages themselves. The enrichment extraction script was created to bridge this gap.\n- **Legal pages empty**: The legal directory downloaded but contained no relevant EHI documentation.\n- **Internal knowledge base links**: The FHIR extensions page references `sites.google.com/onemedical.com/technology-knowledge-base/...` for ML extension definitions — these are internal and not publicly accessible.\n- **No sample data**: Despite documenting a complex FHIR bundle export format, no sample JSON or XML files are provided anywhere on the site.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
